teller/base.py
1:"""Base handler protocol for teller.
2-
3:All handlers must inherit from BaseHandler and implement the required methods.
4:"""
5-
6:from abc import ABC, abstractmethod
7-
8-
9:class BaseHandler(ABC):
10:    """Abstract base class for TTS/output handlers.
11-
12:    Handlers are pluggable backends for teller. Each handler implements
13:    a different output mechanism (espeak, text, debug, etc.).
14-
15:    To create a new handler:
16:    1. Create a .py file in the handlers/ directory
17:    2. Define a class that inherits from BaseHandler
18:    3. Implement all abstract methods
19:    4. The handler will be auto-discovered on import
20:    """
21-
22:    @property
23:    @abstractmethod
24:    def name(self) -> str:
25:        """Unique identifier for this handler.
26-
27:        This is used for registration and lookup. Should be lowercase,
28:        no spaces (e.g., 'espeak', 'text', 'macos_say').
29:        """
30:        pass
31-
32:    @abstractmethod
33:    def speak(self, text: str, speed: int = 270, wait: bool = False) -> None:
34:        """Output the given text.
35-
36:        Args:
37:            text: The text to speak/output.
38:            speed: Speech speed in words per minute (for TTS handlers).
39:                   Non-TTS handlers may ignore this.
40:            wait: If True, block until speech completes.
41:                  If False, return immediately (async).
42:        """
43:        pass
44-
45:    @abstractmethod
46:    def stop(self) -> None:
47:        """Stop any ongoing speech/output.
48-
49:        Should be safe to call even when nothing is playing.
50:        For handlers without interruptible output, this is a no-op.
51:        """
52:        pass

projects/tome-of-lore/STRATEGIC_IMPROVEMENT_BRIEF.md
1:# Strategic Improvement Brief - Tome of Lore Agent Iterability
2-
3:*Synthesized from AGENT_ITERABILITY_ASSESSMENT.md*
4:*Date: 2026-01-21*
5-
6:## Strategic Context
7-
8:The Tome of Lore has **solid foundations for agent iteration** but needs **feedback loop infrastructure improvements**. The codebase is well-organized with focused modules, clear separation of concerns, and standard tooling - but lacks the integrated verification workflow that enables confident, rapid iteration.
9-
10:## Five Strategic Initiatives
11-
12:These initiatives build on each other, creating compounding improvements to iterability.
13-
14:---
15-
16:### Initiative 1: Enable Reliable Basic Iteration
17:**Timeline:** Immediate (1-2 hours)
18:**Drives:** Momentum, Energy
19:**Removes constraint:** Agents can't iterate confidently today
20-
21:**What:**
22:- Add `make check` target combining lint + test
23:- Add CONTRIBUTING.md documenting the iteration loop
24:- Ensure `make test` runs without TTS hardware dependency
25-
26:**Why this first:**
27:Without a reliable "did my change work?" signal, iteration is uncertain and slow. This creates the foundation everything else builds on. The energy here is removing friction - making the basic loop work.
28-
29:**Concrete deliverable:**
30:```makefile
31:check: lint test
32:	@echo "✓ All checks passed"
33:```
34-
35:---
36-
37:### Initiative 2: Add Type-Level Feedback
38:**Timeline:** Short-term (2-4 hours)
39:**Drives:** Clarity, Structure
40:**Removes constraint:** Only runtime errors caught
41-
42:**What:**
43:- Configure mypy with strict mode
44:- Add type checking to `make check`
45:- Fix any revealed type issues
46-
47:**Why this second:**
48:Once the basic loop works, add another feedback layer. Type checking catches errors before tests run, tightening the loop. The structure here is making implicit contracts explicit.
49-
50:**Concrete deliverable:**
51:```toml
52:[tool.mypy]
53:strict = true
54:warn_unreachable = true
55:```
56-
57:---
58-
59:### Initiative 3: Test TTS Integration Without Hardware
60:**Timeline:** Short-term (3-6 hours)
61:**Drives:** Flow, Material
62:**Removes constraint:** Can't test TTS interactions in CI
63-
64:**What:**
65:- Create mock TTS fixtures for pytest
66:- Add integration tests for navigation flows
67:- Add error handling path tests
68-
69:**Why this third:**
70:With basic and type checking working, expand test coverage. The flow here is creating test conditions that match real use without physical dependencies. Material constraint: TTS hardware won't exist in CI.
71-
72:**Concrete deliverable:**
73:```python
74:@pytest.fixture
75:def mock_tts():
76:    with patch('tome.tts.speak') as m:
77:        yield m
78-
79:def test_navigation_speaks(mock_tts, browser):
80:    browser.next()
81:    mock_tts.assert_called_once()
82:```
83-
84:---
85-
86:### Initiative 4: Add Continuous Integration
87:**Timeline:** Medium-term (4-8 hours)
88:**Drives:** Structure, Momentum
89:**Removes constraint:** No automated verification gate
90-
91:**What:**
92:- Add GitHub Actions workflow
93:- Run `make check` on every commit
94:- Add test coverage reporting
95:- Add pre-commit hooks
96-
97:**Why this fourth:**
98:With comprehensive local checks working, automate them. The structure here is creating a forcing function - the gate that ensures quality. Momentum: every commit proves itself.
99-
100:**Concrete deliverable:**
101:```yaml
102:# .github/workflows/check.yml
103:name: Check
104:on: [push, pull_request]
105:jobs:
106:  verify:
107:    runs-on: ubuntu-latest
108:    steps:
109:      - uses: actions/checkout@v3
110:      - uses: actions/setup-python@v4
111:      - run: make check
112:```
113-
114:---
115-
116:### Initiative 5: Document Architecture for Agent Context
117:**Timeline:** Medium-term (3-5 hours)
118:**Drives:** Clarity, Will
119:**Removes constraint:** Agents must infer architecture from code
120-
121:**What:**
122:- Create ARCHITECTURE.md explaining design decisions
123:- Document module responsibilities and boundaries
124:- Add decision records (ADRs) for key choices
125:- Explain TTS abstraction and state management
126-
127:**Why this fifth:**
128:With all feedback loops working, add the context layer. The clarity here is making the *why* explicit, not just the *what*. Will: this enables agents to make aligned changes, not just safe ones.
129-
130:**Concrete deliverable:**
131:```markdown
132:# Architecture
133-
134:## Core Abstractions
135:- **Browser**: State machine for navigation
136:- **TTS**: Speech output abstraction
137:- **Bindings**: Keyboard → action mapping
138:- **Config**: User preferences
139-
140:## Key Decisions
141:- [ADR-001] State-based navigation over command parsing
142:- [ADR-002] TTS abstraction for testing
143:```
144-
145:---
146-
147:## Strategic Synthesis
148-
149:### How They Compound
150-
151:1. **Basic loop** (Initiative 1) enables experimentation
152:2. **Type checking** (Initiative 2) catches more errors earlier
153:3. **TTS mocking** (Initiative 3) enables comprehensive testing
154:4. **CI** (Initiative 4) enforces quality automatically
155:5. **Architecture docs** (Initiative 5) enable aligned evolution
156-
157:Each layer builds on the previous. Without #1, #2 has nothing to integrate with. Without #2 and #3, #4 would just automate incomplete checks. Without #1-4 working, #5 is just documentation.
158-
159:### The Tension Points
160-
161:**Energy vs. Structure:** Initiative 1 wants momentum ("just make it work"), but Initiatives 2 and 4 want rigor. Resolution: Get basic momentum first, then add structure.
162-
163:**Flow vs. Material:** Initiative 3 wants intuitive TTS testing, but TTS hardware is a material constraint. Resolution: Mock the constraint away.
164-
165:**Will vs. Clarity:** Initiative 5 wants to enable aligned changes (will), which requires making architecture explicit (clarity). They reinforce.
166-
167:### Success Metrics
168-
169:**After Initiative 1:**
170:- Agent can run `make check` and trust the result
171:- Iteration time: ~30 seconds (edit → verify)
172-
173:**After Initiative 3:**
174:- 80%+ test coverage including TTS interactions
175:- Tests run without hardware dependencies
176-
177:**After Initiative 4:**
178:- Every commit auto-verified
179:- Pre-commit hooks prevent broken commits
180-
181:**After Initiative 5:**
182:- Agent can make architectural changes confidently
183:- New contributors understand design in <30 minutes
184-
185:### Next Actions
186-
187:**Immediate:** Start Initiative 1 - add `make check` and CONTRIBUTING.md
188:**Sequence:** Complete 1 → 2 → 3 → 4 → 5 in order
189:**Time to full iterability:** ~20-30 hours of focused work
190-
191:---
192-
193:## Conclusion
194-
195:The Tome of Lore is **already iterable** - but these five initiatives transform it from "possible to iterate" to "confident agent-driven development." The foundation is good. The path is clear. The improvements compound.
196-
197:Start with Initiative 1. Everything else follows.

teller/discovery.py
1:"""Handler discovery for teller.
2-
3:Auto-discovers and registers handlers from the handlers/ directory.
4:Drop a .py file with a BaseHandler subclass and it gets picked up.
5:"""
6-
7:import importlib
8:import importlib.util
9:import logging
10:from pathlib import Path
11:from typing import Dict, Type
12-
13:from .base import BaseHandler
14-
15:logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
16-
17-
18:def discover_handlers(handlers_dir: Path = None) -> tuple[Dict[str, BaseHandler], Dict[str, type]]:
19:    """Discover and instantiate handlers from a directory.
20-
21:    Scans the given directory for .py files, imports them, and looks
22:    for classes that inherit from BaseHandler. Each valid handler is
23:    instantiated and registered under its name property.
24-
25:    Args:
26:        handlers_dir: Directory to scan. Defaults to ./handlers/ relative
27:                      to this module.
28-
29:    Returns:
30:        Tuple of (handler instances dict, handler classes dict).
31:    """
32:    if handlers_dir is None:
33:        handlers_dir = Path(__file__).parent / "handlers"
34-
35:    handlers: Dict[str, BaseHandler] = {}
36:    handler_classes: Dict[str, type] = {}
37-
38:    if not handlers_dir.exists():
39:        logger.warning(f"[teller] Handlers directory not found: {handlers_dir}")
40:        return handlers, handler_classes
41-
42:    for file_path in handlers_dir.glob("*.py"):
43:        # Skip __init__.py and private files
44:        if file_path.stem.startswith("_"):
45:            continue
46-
47:        try:
48:            # Load the module
49:            module = _load_module_from_path(file_path)
50:            if module is None:
51:                continue
52-
53:            # Find BaseHandler subclasses
54:            for item_name in dir(module):
55:                if item_name.startswith("_"):
56:                    continue
57-
58:                item = getattr(module, item_name)
59-
60:                # Check if it's a class that inherits from BaseHandler
61:                if (isinstance(item, type) and
62:                    issubclass(item, BaseHandler) and
63:                    item is not BaseHandler):
64-
65:                    try:
66:                        # Instantiate and register
67:                        instance = item()
68:                        handler_name = instance.name
69:                        handlers[handler_name] = instance
70:                        handler_classes[handler_name] = item
71:                        logger.debug(f"[teller] Registered handler: {handler_name}")
72:                    except Exception as e:
73:                        logger.warning(
74:                            f"[teller] Failed to instantiate handler "
75:                            f"{item_name} from {file_path}: {e}"
76:                        )
77-
78:        except Exception as e:
79:            logger.warning(f"[teller] Failed to load {file_path}: {e}")
80-
81:    return handlers, handler_classes
82-
83-
84:def _load_module_from_path(file_path: Path):
85:    """Load a Python module from a file path.
86-
87:    Args:
88:        file_path: Path to the .py file.
89-
90:    Returns:
91:        The loaded module, or None if loading failed.
92:    """
93:    module_name = f"teller.handlers.{file_path.stem}"
94-
95:    try:
96:        spec = importlib.util.spec_from_file_location(module_name, file_path)
97:        if spec is None or spec.loader is None:
98:            return None
99-
100:        module = importlib.util.module_from_spec(spec)
101:        spec.loader.exec_module(module)
102:        return module
103-
104:    except Exception as e:
105:        logger.warning(f"[teller] Import error for {file_path}: {e}")
106:        return None

teller/__init__.py
1:"""teller - Pluggable TTS/output primitive.
2-
3:A flexible text-to-speech and output library with pluggable handlers.
4:Designed for reuse across projects.
5-
6:Basic usage:
7:    from teller import speak, announce, stop
8-
9:    speak("Hello world")          # Uses default handler
10:    announce("Urgent message")    # Stops current, then speaks
11:    stop()                        # Interrupt speech
12-
13:Handler management:
14:    from teller import get_handler, list_handlers, set_default_handler
15-
16:    list_handlers()               # ['espeak', 'text', 'debug']
17:    set_default_handler('text')   # Switch to text output
18:    handler = get_handler('debug')
19:    handler.speak("Direct call")
20-
21:Adding handlers:
22:    Drop a .py file in teller/handlers/ with a class that inherits
23:    from BaseHandler. It will be auto-discovered on import.
24:"""
25-
26:import logging
27:from typing import Dict, List, Optional
28-
29:from .base import BaseHandler
30:from .discovery import discover_handlers
31-
32:logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
33-
34:# Handler registry - populated on import
35:_handlers: Dict[str, BaseHandler] = {}
36:_handler_classes: Dict[str, type] = {}
37-
38:# Default handler instance
39:_default_handler: Optional[BaseHandler] = None
40-
41-
42:def _init_handlers() -> None:
43:    """Initialize handler registry via discovery."""
44:    global _handlers, _handler_classes, _default_handler
45-
46:    _handlers, _handler_classes = discover_handlers()
47-
48:    # Set default handler (prefer espeak if available, else text)
49:    if "espeak" in _handlers:
50:        _default_handler = _handlers["espeak"]
51:    elif "text" in _handlers:
52:        _default_handler = _handlers["text"]
53:    elif _handlers:
54:        _default_handler = next(iter(_handlers.values()))
55:    else:
56:        logger.warning("[teller] No handlers discovered")
57-
58-
59:# --- Public API ---
60-
61:def speak(text: str, speed: int = 270, wait: bool = False) -> None:
62:    """Speak/output text using the default handler.
63-
64:    Args:
65:        text: Text to speak.
66:        speed: Speech speed in WPM (for TTS handlers).
67:        wait: If True, block until complete.
68:    """
69:    if _default_handler is None:
70:        logger.error("[teller] No handler available")
71:        return
72:    _default_handler.speak(text, speed=speed, wait=wait)
73-
74-
75:def announce(text: str, speed: int = 270) -> None:
76:    """Interrupt any current speech and speak immediately.
77-
78:    Useful for urgent messages that shouldn't wait.
79-
80:    Args:
81:        text: Text to speak.
82:        speed: Speech speed in WPM.
83:    """
84:    if _default_handler is None:
85:        logger.error("[teller] No handler available")
86:        return
87:    _default_handler.stop()
88:    _default_handler.speak(text, speed=speed, wait=False)
89-
90-
91:def stop() -> None:
92:    """Stop any ongoing speech."""
93:    if _default_handler is not None:
94:        _default_handler.stop()
95-
96-
97:def get_handler(name: str) -> BaseHandler:
98:    """Get a handler by name.
99-
100:    Args:
101:        name: Handler name (e.g., 'espeak', 'text', 'debug').
102-
103:    Returns:
104:        A fresh handler instance.
105-
106:    Raises:
107:        KeyError: If handler not found.
108:    """
109:    if name not in _handler_classes:
110:        available = ", ".join(_handler_classes.keys()) if _handler_classes else "none"
111:        raise KeyError(f"Handler '{name}' not found. Available: {available}")
112:    # Return fresh instance to avoid shared mutable state
113:    return _handler_classes[name]()
114-
115-
116:def list_handlers() -> List[str]:
117:    """List all available handler names.
118-
119:    Returns:
120:        List of handler names.
121:    """
122:    return list(_handlers.keys())
123-
124-
125:def set_default_handler(name: str) -> None:
126:    """Set the default handler used by speak() and announce().
127-
128:    Args:
129:        name: Handler name.
130-
131:    Raises:
132:        KeyError: If handler not found.
133:    """
134:    global _default_handler
135:    if name not in _handlers:
136:        available = ", ".join(_handlers.keys()) if _handlers else "none"
137:        raise KeyError(f"Handler '{name}' not found. Available: {available}")
138:    _default_handler = _handlers[name]
139-
140-
141:# Initialize on import
142:_init_handlers()
143-
144-
145:__all__ = [
146:    "speak",
147:    "announce",
148:    "stop",
149:    "get_handler",
150:    "list_handlers",
151:    "set_default_handler",
152:    "BaseHandler",
153:]

projects/tome-of-lore/AGENT_ITERABILITY_ASSESSMENT.md
1:# Agent Iterability Assessment - Tome of Lore
2-
3:*Phase 4 Finding for tome-improvement site*
4:*Date: Assessment completed, skein unavailable for posting*
5-
6:## Executive Summary
7-
8:**Can an agent iterate on this codebase?** YES - with caveats.
9-
10:The Tome of Lore codebase has solid foundations for agent iteration but needs some improvements to the feedback loop infrastructure.
11-
12:## Feedback Loops Analysis
13-
14:### 1. Test Suite ✅ Good Foundation
15-
16:**What exists:**
17:- pytest-based test suite in `tests/` directory
18:- Unit tests for core components (config, state, bindings)
19:- Test fixtures and proper test organization
20:- Tests can be run with `pytest` or `make test`
21-
22:**Agent-friendliness:**
23:- Tests are focused and provide clear pass/fail signals
24:- Good coverage of core functionality
25:- Test output is parseable
26-
27:**Gaps:**
28:- No integration tests for TTS interaction
29:- No end-to-end tests for navigation flows
30:- Missing tests for error handling paths
31-
32:### 2. Build System ⚠️ Partial
33-
34:**What exists:**
35:- `Makefile` with common targets
36:- `pyproject.toml` for package configuration
37:- Standard Python project structure
38-
39:**Agent-friendliness:**
40:- `make test` provides quick feedback
41:- `make lint` available for style checking
42:- Clear entry points
43-
44:**Gaps:**
45:- No CI/CD configuration visible
46:- No automated build verification
47:- Missing `make check` or `make verify` all-in-one target
48-
49:### 3. Linting ✅ Available
50-
51:**What exists:**
52:- Ruff configured in `pyproject.toml`
53:- `make lint` target
54:- Type hints present in codebase
55-
56:**Agent-friendliness:**
57:- Fast feedback on style issues
58:- Clear error messages
59:- Auto-fix capability with ruff
60-
61:**Gaps:**
62:- No pre-commit hooks configured
63:- mypy not configured for type checking
64:- No strict mode enforcement
65-
66:### 4. Documentation 📝 Mixed
67-
68:**What exists:**
69:- README.md with project overview
70:- Inline docstrings in some modules
71:- Architecture evident from directory structure
72-
73:**Gaps:**
74:- No ARCHITECTURE.md or CONTRIBUTING.md
75:- API documentation not generated
76:- No decision records (ADRs)
77-
78:## Agent Iteration Patterns
79-
80:### What Works Well
81-
82:1. **Small, focused modules** - Changes are isolated
83:2. **Clear separation of concerns** - Config, state, UI, TTS are distinct
84:3. **Test coverage for core** - Regressions caught quickly
85:4. **Standard tooling** - pytest, ruff, make are familiar
86-
87:### What Would Help
88-
89:1. **Add `make check` target** - Run all verification in one command:
90:   ```makefile
91:   check: lint test
92:   	echo "All checks passed"
93:   ```
94-
95:2. **Add type checking** - Configure mypy:
96:   ```toml
97:   [tool.mypy]
98:   strict = true
99:   ```
100-
101:3. **Add integration test scaffolding** - Mock TTS for testing:
102:   ```python
103:   @pytest.fixture
104:   def mock_tts():
105:       with patch('tome.tts.speak') as m:
106:           yield m
107:   ```
108-
109:4. **Document the iteration loop** - Add CONTRIBUTING.md:
110:   ```markdown
111:   ## Making Changes
112:   1. Run `make check` before starting
113:   2. Make your changes
114:   3. Run `make check` again
115:   4. All tests must pass
116:   ```
117-
118:## Recommended Improvements
119-
120:### Priority 1: Immediate (enables basic iteration)
121:- [ ] Add `make check` all-in-one target
122:- [ ] Add CONTRIBUTING.md with iteration workflow
123:- [ ] Ensure `make test` runs reliably without TTS hardware
124-
125:### Priority 2: Short-term (improves feedback quality)
126:- [ ] Configure mypy for type checking
127:- [ ] Add mock TTS for integration tests
128:- [ ] Add test coverage reporting
129-
130:### Priority 3: Medium-term (enables confident iteration)
131:- [ ] Add CI/CD configuration (GitHub Actions)
132:- [ ] Add pre-commit hooks
133:- [ ] Create ARCHITECTURE.md
134-
135:## Conclusion
136-
137:An agent CAN iterate on this codebase today. The core feedback loop (edit → test → verify) exists and works. However, the experience would be significantly improved with:
138-
139:1. A single `make check` command for full verification
140:2. Better test isolation from TTS hardware
141:3. Type checking enabled
142-
143:The codebase follows good practices and is well-organized. With the suggested improvements, it would be an excellent target for agent-driven development.

teller/handlers/espeak.py
1:"""Espeak handler - real TTS via espeak/espeak-ng subprocess.
2-
3:Uses the system espeak installation to synthesize and play speech.
4:Works with both espeak and espeak-ng.
5:"""
6-
7:import subprocess
8:import shutil
9:import logging
10:from typing import Optional
11-
12:from teller.base import BaseHandler
13-
14:logger = logging.getLogger(__name__)
15-
16-
17:class EspeakHandler(BaseHandler):
18:    """Handler that uses espeak for text-to-speech."""
19-
20:    def __init__(self):
21:        self._process: Optional[subprocess.Popen] = None
22:        self._espeak_cmd = self._find_espeak()
23-
24:    @property
25:    def name(self) -> str:
26:        return "espeak"
27-
28:    def _find_espeak(self) -> Optional[str]:
29:        """Find espeak or espeak-ng executable."""
30:        for cmd in ["espeak-ng", "espeak"]:
31:            if shutil.which(cmd):
32:                return cmd
33:        logger.warning("[teller/espeak] espeak not found on system")
34:        return None
35-
36:    def speak(self, text: str, speed: int = 270, wait: bool = False) -> None:
37:        """Speak text using espeak.
38-
39:        Args:
40:            text: Text to speak.
41:            speed: Words per minute (espeak -s flag).
42:            wait: If True, block until speech completes.
43:        """
44:        if not self._espeak_cmd:
45:            logger.error("[teller/espeak] espeak not available")
46:            return
47-
48:        # Kill any ongoing speech first
49:        self.stop()
50-
51:        if not text:
52:            return
53-
54:        # Build command
55:        # -s: speed in WPM
56:        # -z: no final pause
57:        cmd = [self._espeak_cmd, f"-s{speed}", "-z", text]
58-
59:        try:
60:            if wait:
61:                subprocess.call(cmd)
62:            else:
63:                self._process = subprocess.Popen(cmd)
64:        except Exception as e:
65:            logger.error(f"[teller/espeak] Failed to speak: {e}")
66-
67:    def stop(self) -> None:
68:        """Stop any ongoing speech."""
69:        # Terminate our tracked process if running
70:        if self._process is not None:
71:            try:
72:                self._process.terminate()
73:                self._process.wait(timeout=0.5)
74:            except Exception:
75:                try:
76:                    self._process.kill()
77:                except Exception:
78:                    pass
79:            self._process = None

305:    def test_stop_doesnt_kill_external_espeak(self):
306:        """Bug fix: stop() should only kill teller's process, not all espeak."""
307:        import subprocess
308:        import time
309:        import shutil
310:        from teller import get_handler
311:        
312:        if not shutil.which('espeak-ng') and not shutil.which('espeak'):
313:            pytest.skip("espeak not installed")
314:        
315:        # Start external espeak process
316:        ext_proc = subprocess.Popen(['espeak-ng', '-s270', '-z', 'external'])
317:        time.sleep(0.1)
318:        
319:        # Use teller
320:        handler = get_handler('espeak')
321:        handler.speak('teller', wait=False)
322:        time.sleep(0.1)
323:        
324:        # Stop teller speech
325:        handler.stop()
326:        time.sleep(0.1)
327:        
328:        # External process should still be alive
329:        assert ext_proc.poll() is None, "stop() killed external espeak process"
330:        ext_proc.kill()

332:    def test_empty_speak_calls_stop(self):
333:        """Bug fix: speak("") should call stop() like any other speak."""
334:        from teller import get_handler
335:        from unittest.mock import MagicMock, patch
336:        
337:        handler = get_handler('espeak')
338:        
339:        # Patch stop to track if it's called

341:        stop_called = []
342:        
343:        def track_stop():
344:            stop_called.append(True)
345:            original_stop()
346:        
347:        handler.stop = track_stop

353:    def test_set_default_handler_uses_singleton(self):

355:        from teller import set_default_handler
356:        import teller
357:        
358:        # Get the singleton instance
359:        original = teller._handlers['text']
360:        
361:        # Set as default
362:        set_default_handler('text')
363:        
364:        # Should be the same instance
365:        assert teller._default_handler is original, \
366:            "set_default_handler created new instance instead of using singleton"
